                    - generic [ref=e1292]: G6P, glucose 6-phosphate; 6PGL, 6-phosphate glucono-lactone; 6PG, 6-phosphate gluconate; 2K3DPG, 2-keto-3-deoxy-6-phosphate gluconic acid; Ru5P, ribulose 5-phosphate; R5P, ribose 5-phosphate; X5P, xylulose 5-phosphate; F-1,6-BP, fructose 1,6-bisphosphate; S7P, sedoheptulose 7-phosphate; E4P, erythrose 4-phosphate; F6P, fructose 6-phosphate; 1,3-BPG, 1,3-bisphosphoglycerate; 2-PG, 2-phosphoglycerate; PYR, pyruvate.
                  - generic [ref=e1293]:
                    - heading "3.3. Construction of a novel transhydrogenase system for integrating both redox and energy metabolism" [level=3] [ref=e1294]
                    - generic [ref=e1295]:
                      - generic [ref=e1296]:
                        - text: Intracellular metabolic reactions required rigorous cofactor and energy balance to support cell growth and biosynthesis. The above experiments had shown that imbalances in both NAD(
                        - emphasis [ref=e1297]: P
                        - text: )H and NAD(P)
                        - superscript [ref=e1298]: +
                        - text: levels disturbed redox homeostasis and limited biomass accumulation. Thus, we designed a synthetic transhydrogenase cycling system that links NADPH consumption to NADH and ATP regeneration, thereby integrating both redox and energy metabolism. This system enabled surplus NADPH convert into NADH
                        - emphasis [ref=e1299]: via
                        - text: heterologous glutamate dehydrogenases, with NADH subsequently used for ATP production
                        - emphasis [ref=e1300]: via
                        - text: the respiratory chain. In addition to providing energy, ATP also functions as a cofactor for pantothenate synthetase (PanC), which supported D-PA synthesis [
                        - link "19" [ref=e1301] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0095"
                          - generic [ref=e1302]: "19"
                        - text: "]. The genetic modules involved were shown in"
                        - link "Fig. 3" [ref=e1303] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0015"
                          - generic [ref=e1304]: Fig. 3
                        - text: A.
                      - figure [ref=e1305]:
                        - generic [ref=e1306]:
                          - img "Fig. 3"
                          - list [ref=e1307]:
                            - listitem [ref=e1308]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download high-res image (2MB)" [ref=e1309] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr3_lrg.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1311]: "Download: Download high-res image (2MB)"
                            - listitem [ref=e1312]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download full-size image" [ref=e1313] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr3.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1315]: "Download: Download full-size image"
                        - paragraph [ref=e1318]:
                          - text: Fig. 3. Introducing a novel heterologous transhydrogenase system to improve energy coupling. The nomenclature ‘
                          - emphasis [ref=e1319]: Scgdh1
                          - text: +
                          - emphasis [ref=e1320]: Scgdh2
                          - text: ’ in the figure for example, referred to strain DPAW10C11 derived from ‘DPAW10C10-3 derivative, Ptrc-
                          - emphasis [ref=e1321]: Scgdh1
                          - text: /Ptrc-
                          - emphasis [ref=e1322]: Scgdh2’
                          - text: and ‘Δ’ represented gene knockout. ‘P
                          - subscript [ref=e1323]:
                            - emphasis [ref=e1324]: flic
                          - text: "-"
                          - emphasis [ref=e1325]: icd
                          - text: ’ indicated the promoter of the
                          - emphasis [ref=e1326]: icd
                          - text: gene was mediated by flic.
                    - generic [ref=e1327]:
                      - text: Primarily, to eliminate the negative effects caused by superfluous NADPH, we overexpressed glutamate dehydrogenases GDH1 and GDH2 derived from
                      - emphasis [ref=e1328]: S. cerevisiae
                      - text: "["
                      - link "47" [ref=e1329] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0235"
                        - generic [ref=e1330]: "47"
                      - text: "]. The irreversible transhydrogenation pathway converted one molecule of NADPH (from Zwf and Gnd) into another molecule of NADH. It could be observed that intracellular NADPH concentration decreased while NADH concentration significantly increased to 71.58 pmol/well in DPAW10C11 ("
                      - link "Fig. 3" [ref=e1331] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0015"
                        - generic [ref=e1332]: Fig. 3
                      - text: B), confirming the efficient operation of the transhydrogenation cycle. Simultaneously, D-PA titer reached 5.83 g/L with improved D-PA/OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1333]: "600"
                      - text: ratio of 1.04. According to FBA and FVA simulation, the D-PA production under ATP surplus conditions could be 2.79 times that of ATP deficiency, which demonstrated that once NADPH limitation was relieved, the supply of ATP emerged as the next factor influencing D-PA production. On the other hand, elevated NADH concentrations cause electron transport chain interference disruption and glycolysis feedback inhibition, adversely affecting cell growth [
                      - link "48" [ref=e1334] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0240"
                        - generic [ref=e1335]: "48"
                      - text: "]. In natural respiration, excess NADH is oxidized"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1336]: via
                      - text: the electron transport chain [
                      - link "49" [ref=e1337] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0245"
                        - generic [ref=e1338]: "49"
                      - text: ","
                      - link "50" [ref=e1339] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0250"
                        - generic [ref=e1340]: "50"
                      - text: "]. To dissipate cytoplasmic NADH, we then constructed a respiratory bypass system incorporating several components from"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1341]: S. cerevisiae
                      - text: "["
                      - link "51" [ref=e1342] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0255"
                        - generic [ref=e1343]: "51"
                      - text: "] (these genes had undergone codon optimization to improve their expression in"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1344]: E. coli
                      - text: ).
                    - generic [ref=e1345]:
                      - text: For the first module, the single-subunit NADH dehydrogenase NDI1 from
                      - emphasis [ref=e1346]: S. cerevisiae
                      - text: was expressed to transfer NADH electrons (derived from TCA) to ubiquinone within the respiratory chain [
                      - link "51" [ref=e1347] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0255"
                        - generic [ref=e1348]: "51"
                      - text: "]. As a result, part of NADH was converted to promote electronic transmission for DPAW10C11–1, which displayed an increase in ATP concentration (2.49 μmol/g) and a decrease in NADH concentration (66.57 pmol/well). At the same time, D-PA titer arrived at 5.99 g/L."
                    - generic [ref=e1349]:
                      - text: In parallel, coenzyme Q (CoQ) is used as an electron transport protein for NADH dehydrogenase and as a cytochrome C reductase in succinate dehydrogenase. According to previous reports, introduction of chorismate pyruvate lyase UbiCA can enhance CoQ synthesis [
                      - link "52" [ref=e1350] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0260"
                        - generic [ref=e1351]: "52"
                      - text: "]. Therefore, we introduced ubiCA into DPAW10C11–1 to construct DPAW10C12, which slightly enhanced the conversion from NADH (64.75 pmol/well) to ATP (2.75 μmol/g), strengthening the production of D-PA (6.11 g/L)."
                    - generic [ref=e1352]:
                      - text: To further relieve NADH burden, external NADH dehydrogenases NDE1 and NDE2 were expressed to connect cytoplasmic NADH to the electron transfer chain. Therefore, this integrated exogenous respiratory chain enabled cytosolic NADH to feed into oxidative phosphorylation [
                      - link "51" [ref=e1353] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0255"
                        - generic [ref=e1354]: "51"
                      - text: "], reducing NADH accumulation to 57.31 pmol/well with satisfactory ATP concentration (3.11 μmol/g) and D-PA production (6.25 g/L) in DPAW10C13. At this point, the NADH generated from transhydrogenation (mediated by GDH1 and GDH2) was transferred into the respiratory chain"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1355]: via
                      - text: NDI1 and NDE1/NDE2. However, although these strategies partially relieved NAD(
                      - emphasis [ref=e1356]: P
                      - text: )H burden and improved ATP generation, the growth of DPAW10C13 (OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1357]: "600"
                      - text: 6.19) remained still lower than that of DPAW10C10–3 (OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1358]: "600"
                      - text: 6.46), indicating that further adjustments were needed to achieve a more balanced redox state.
                    - generic [ref=e1359]:
                      - text: In metabolic systems, TCA serves as a central hub for both redox balance and metabolic flux distributions. Approximately 25 % of cellular NAD(
                      - emphasis [ref=e1360]: P
                      - text: )H originates from isocitrate dehydrogenase (IDH) in TCA [
                      - link "42" [ref=e1361] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0210"
                        - generic [ref=e1362]: "42"
                      - text: "]. While previous studies have primarily focused on the competition between the TCA cycle and D-PA biosynthesis for the precursor pyruvate, the regulation of cofactor metabolism in this context has not been systematically investigated ["
                      - link "19" [ref=e1363] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0095"
                        - generic [ref=e1364]: "19"
                      - text: "]. Thus, to balance cofactor distribution and carbon flux,"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1365]: icd
                      - text: expression was modulated using a dynamic promoter
                      - emphasis [ref=e1366]: P
                      - subscript [ref=e1367]:
                        - emphasis [ref=e1368]: flic
                      - text: (derived from
                      - emphasis [ref=e1369]: fliC
                      - text: ", active in the logarithmic phase and downregulated in the stationary phase), which has been reported to drive"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1370]: icd
                      - text: at a reduced strength (approximately 10 % of its native level) in the stationary phase [
                      - link "51" [ref=e1371] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0255"
                        - generic [ref=e1372]: "51"
                      - text: "], thereby providing self-adjustment based on growth phase. This strategy, rather than relying on simple overexpression or knockdown, mitigated NAD("
                      - emphasis [ref=e1373]: P
                      - text: )H overproduction during late-stage cultivation and alleviated redox overload [
                      - link "53" [ref=e1374] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0265"
                        - generic [ref=e1375]: "53"
                      - text: "]. Results shown in"
                      - link "Fig. 3" [ref=e1376] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0015"
                        - generic [ref=e1377]: Fig. 3
                      - text: B demonstrated that DPAW10C14 maintained ATP levels (2.94 μmol/g) and achieved a peak D-PA titer of 6.61 g/L, accompanied by increased precursor concentrations (pyruvate from 0.23 g/L in DPAW10C13 to 0.49 g/L in DPAW10C14, and α-ketoisovalerate from 0.33 to 0.39 g/L) (Fig. S3), suggesting that higher precursor availability may enhance D-PA production. The OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1378]: "600"
                      - text: of DPAW10C14 increased to 6.38, likely supported by α-ketoisovalerate and pyruvate serving as precursors for branched-chain and other amino acids [
                      - link "54" [ref=e1379] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0270"
                        - generic [ref=e1380]: "54"
                      - text: ","
                      - link "55" [ref=e1381] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0275"
                        - generic [ref=e1382]: "55"
                      - text: "], thereby promoting cell growth. Meanwhile, NADPH and NADH levels were maintained at 61.41 pmol/well and 49.91 pmol/well, respectively, indicating successful rebalancing of cofactor and energy metabolism."
                    - generic [ref=e1383]:
                      - text: So far, these efforts constructed a synthetic redox-energy network that integrated transhydrogenation and respiratory dissipation with dynamic TCA regulation. The strategy supplied both reducing power and ATP while redirecting pyruvate toward D-PA biosynthesis, which offered a scalable framework for the efficient production of other value-added compounds requiring high NAD(
                      - emphasis [ref=e1384]: P
                      - text: )H input or pyruvate precursors.
                    - generic [ref=e1385]:
                      - text: (A) Using the respiratory chain system derived from
                      - emphasis [ref=e1386]: S. cerevisiae
                      - text: and the electron transport chain of
                      - emphasis [ref=e1387]: E. coli
                      - text: ", cofactors in the cytoplasm and TCA could be mutually transformed and utilized. Red genes indicated overexpression while blue genes meant repression. The black solid line represented a direct action while the black dashed line represented multi-step reaction. (B) Changes of D-PA production, cell growth and D-PA/OD"
                      - subscript [ref=e1388]: "600"
                      - text: as well as values of NAD
                      - superscript [ref=e1389]: +
                      - text: ", NADH, NADP"
                      - superscript [ref=e1390]: +
                      - text: ", NADPH and ATP by engineering transhydrogenation systems from DPAW10C10–3 to DPAW10C14 in shake flask cultivation."
                    - generic [ref=e1391]: AKG, α-ketoglutaric acid; Glu, glutamate; AcCoA, acetyl-CoA; CIT, citric acid; ICIT, isocitric acid; CoQ, coenzyme Q; pHBA, 4-hydroxybenzoic acid; DPP, ten polyisoprene pyrophosphate.
                  - generic [ref=e1392]:
                    - heading "3.4. Screening of ATP-related genes from electron transport chain and oxidative phosphorylation" [level=3] [ref=e1393]
                    - generic [ref=e1394]:
                      - generic [ref=e1395]:
                        - text: In
                        - emphasis [ref=e1396]: E. coli
                        - text: ", the primary way for ATP generation is achieved by aerobic respiration ["
                        - link "49" [ref=e1397] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0245"
                          - generic [ref=e1398]: "49"
                        - text: "], which involves the electron transport chain, including complex I (NDH-1), the CoQ cycle and cytochrome bo"
                        - subscript [ref=e1399]: "3"
                        - text: oxidase, as well as oxidative phosphorylation with ATP synthase [
                        - link "56" [ref=e1400] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0280"
                          - generic [ref=e1401]: "56"
                        - text: "] ("
                        - link "Fig. 4" [ref=e1402] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0020"
                          - generic [ref=e1403]: Fig. 4
                        - text: A). Although earlier efforts were made to construct a transhydrogenation system and modified its upstream components such as complex I and CoQ, these changes did not completely overcome ATP limitation, as exogenous ATP supplementation still further enhanced D-PA biosynthesis to 6.84 g/L in DPAW10C14 (Fig. S4A). Given that NADH supplementation no longer benefited D-PA synthesis, we shifted our focus to directly enhancing the ATP-producing module (ATP synthase).
                      - figure [ref=e1404]:
                        - generic [ref=e1405]:
                          - img "Fig. 4"
                          - list [ref=e1406]:
                            - listitem [ref=e1407]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download high-res image (651KB)" [ref=e1408] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr4_lrg.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1410]: "Download: Download high-res image (651KB)"
                            - listitem [ref=e1411]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download full-size image" [ref=e1412] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr4.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1414]: "Download: Download full-size image"
                        - paragraph [ref=e1417]:
                          - text: Fig. 4. Screening ATP synthase and cytochrome oxidase
                          - emphasis [ref=e1418]: via
                          - text: RNAi. The nomenclature ‘
                          - emphasis [ref=e1419]: aptC
                          - text: ’ in the figure for example, referred to strain DPAW10C14–3 derived from ‘DPAW10C14 derivative, ptrc-
                          - emphasis [ref=e1420]: atpC’
                          - text: and ‘Δ’ represented gene knockout.
                    - generic [ref=e1421]:
                      - text: To systematically evaluate the contributions of ATP synthase subunits, we employed a high-efficiency RNA interference (RNAi) strategy
                      - emphasis [ref=e1422]: via
                      - text: plasmid P86, which expressed short interfering RNAs (siRNAs) to interfere target gene expression [
                      - link "57" [ref=e1423] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0285"
                        - generic [ref=e1424]: "57"
                      - text: "]. Each subunit of the ATP synthase complex (ATPA-H) was individually silenced in DPAW10C14. The results showed that RNAi-mediated knockdown of AtpC significantly reduced D-PA production, and inhibition of AtpF or AtpH showed increased D-PA production to 6.73 g/L, while interference with other subunits led to slight changes (Fig. S4B). Besides, these strains exhibited varying degrees of reduced OD"
                      - subscript [ref=e1425]: "600"
                      - text: except for ATPC-silenced strain. This might be attributed to that the inhibition of ATP synthase led to the suppression of energy metabolism, thereby weakening the TCA cycle and reducing the carbon flux competition of D-PA [
                      - link "58" [ref=e1426] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0290"
                        - generic [ref=e1427]: "58"
                      - text: "]. We then performed genomic modifications at these sites identified through RNAi. However, complete knockout of ATP synthase genes"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1428]: atpF
                      - text: and
                      - emphasis [ref=e1429]: atpH
                      - text: ", which had a relatively measurable impact on D-PA through RNAi screening, led to substantial declines in D-PA yield ("
                      - link "Fig. 4" [ref=e1430] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0020"
                        - generic [ref=e1431]: Fig. 4
                      - text: B), confirming that full disruption of ATP synthesis displayed detrimental. Precise regulation of ATP synthesis was more necessary for balancing growth and production. We then overexpressed
                      - emphasis [ref=e1432]: atpC
                      - text: to construct DPAW10C14–3, which obtained modestly increased intracellular ATP level (reached 3.11 μmol/g in
                      - link "Fig. 4" [ref=e1433] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0020"
                        - generic [ref=e1434]: Fig. 4
                      - text: C) and D-PA production (6.71 g/L) despite a reduction in biomass (
                      - link "Fig. 4" [ref=e1435] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0020"
                        - generic [ref=e1436]: Fig. 4
                      - text: B). This effect can be attributed to the ε-subunit (encoded by
                      - emphasis [ref=e1437]: atpC
                      - text: ), which restrains excessive ATP hydrolysis by regulating the F₁F₀-ATP synthase complex [
                      - link "59" [ref=e1438] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0295"
                        - generic [ref=e1439]: "59"
                      - text: "], and thereby contributes to maintaining metabolic balance for D-PA synthesis."
                    - generic [ref=e1440]:
                      - text: Additionally, to further enhance ATP levels, the contributions of electron transport chain (ETC) components and four structure genes of the cytochrome bo
                      - subscript [ref=e1441]: "3"
                      - text: oxidase complex (CyoA-D) were taken into considerations for their roles in respiration-coupled ATP generation. Among these, only CyoA (responsible for electron transfer) and CyoB (responsible for xygen reduction/proton translocation), affected by RNAi, exhibited detectable effects on D-PA productions (Fig. S4C) [
                      - link "60" [ref=e1442] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0300"
                        - generic [ref=e1443]: "60"
                      - text: "]. However, replacing the native promoter of"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1444]: cyoAB
                      - text: with the trc promoter led to negligible changes in ATP (
                      - link "Fig. 4" [ref=e1445] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0020"
                        - generic [ref=e1446]: Fig. 4
                      - text: C) and D-PA levels (
                      - link "Fig. 4" [ref=e1447] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0020"
                        - generic [ref=e1448]: Fig. 4
                      - text: B), which could be due to the dominance of cytochrome
                      - emphasis [ref=e1449]: bd
                      - text: oxidase (CydAB) under high‑oxygen conditions [
                      - link "61" [ref=e1450] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0305"
                        - generic [ref=e1451]: "61"
                      - text: "] instead of bo"
                      - subscript [ref=e1452]: "3"
                      - text: oxidase (CyoAB) in
                      - emphasis [ref=e1453]: E. coli
                      - text: .
                    - generic [ref=e1454]:
                      - text: Taken together, these findings demonstrated that, although engineering oxidative phosphorylation and the electron transport chain could increase ATP supply and thereby enhance D-PA production theoretically, more precise studies and fine-tuned regulation of the expression of the relative enzymes, rather than simple RNAi screening, were required. On the other hand, replacing the native promoter of
                      - emphasis [ref=e1455]: atpC
                      - text: with the trc promoter also offered a balanced solution, providing sufficient ATP for D-PA biosynthesis while maintaining acceptable biomass levels, which also laid a solid foundation for further refinements of energy metabolism.
                    - generic [ref=e1456]:
                      - text: (A) Schematic diagram of electron transport chain and oxidative phosphorylation in
                      - emphasis [ref=e1457]: E. coli
                      - text: . Every time two electrons were transferred through the electron transfer chain, 12H
                      - superscript [ref=e1458]: +
                      - text: were pumped out and each H
                      - superscript [ref=e1459]: +
                      - text: generated 3 ATP when undergoing oxidative phosphorylation. (B) Changes of D-PA production, cell growth for strain DPAW10C14 to DPAW10C14–5 by engineering respiratory chain. (C) Different ATP concentration for strain DPAW10C14 to DPAW10C14–5 by engineering respiratory chain.
                  - generic [ref=e1460]:
                    - heading "3.5. Optimizing the metabolic balance of 5,10-methylenetetrahydrofolate" [level=3] [ref=e1461]
                    - generic [ref=e1462]:
                      - generic [ref=e1463]:
                        - text: In addition to NADPH and ATP, the biosynthesis of D-PA also depends on 5,10-methylene tetrahydrofolate (5,10-MTHF), which serves as a critical one‑carbon unit donor in folate metabolism [
                        - link "22" [ref=e1464] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0110"
                          - generic [ref=e1465]: "22"
                        - text: "]. This cofactor provides the hydroxymethyl group for a key enzyme, ketopantoate hydroxymethyltransferase (PanB), in the D-PA synthesis pathway. In"
                        - emphasis [ref=e1466]: E. coli
                        - text: ", 5,10-MTHF is mainly synthesized by serine hydroxymethyltransferase (GlyA), which transfers the hydroxymethyl group from l-serine to tetrahydrofolate (THF), yielding 5,10-MTHF and glycine, while the glycine cleavage system (GcvTHP and Lpd) provides a minor contribution ["
                        - link "22" [ref=e1467] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0110"
                          - generic [ref=e1468]: "22"
                        - text: "]. To improve one‑carbon unit regeneration, the serine-glycine interconversion was systematically engineered ("
                        - link "Fig. 5" [ref=e1469] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0025"
                          - generic [ref=e1470]: Fig. 5
                        - text: A).
                      - figure [ref=e1471]:
                        - generic [ref=e1472]:
                          - img "Fig. 5"
                          - list [ref=e1473]:
                            - listitem [ref=e1474]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download high-res image (1MB)" [ref=e1475] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr5_lrg.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1477]: "Download: Download high-res image (1MB)"
                            - listitem [ref=e1478]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download full-size image" [ref=e1479] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr5.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1481]: "Download: Download full-size image"
                        - paragraph [ref=e1484]:
                          - text: Fig. 5. Transforming the Serine-Glycine Cycle to promote the synthesis of one carbon unit 5,10-methyltetrahydrofolic acid. The nomenclature ‘
                          - emphasis [ref=e1485]: serA
                          - superscript [ref=e1486]: H344A/N346A/D364A
                          - text: ’ in the figure for example, referred to strain DPAW10C15 derived from ‘DPAW10C14-3 derivative, Ptrc-
                          - emphasis [ref=e1487]: serA
                          - superscript [ref=e1488]: H344A/N346A/D364A
                          - emphasis [ref=e1489]: ’
                          - text: and the superscripts ATG, GTG, and TTG indicated changes in the starting codon.
                    - generic [ref=e1490]:
                      - text: Building on prior studies revealing that appropriate exogenous l-serine supplementation enhanced D-PA synthesis [
                      - link "22" [ref=e1491] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0110"
                        - generic [ref=e1492]: "22"
                      - text: "], first efforts were made to target endogenous serine biosynthesis. In"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1493]: E. coli
                      - text: ", 3-phosphoglycerate (3-PG) is converted to l-serine through sequential reactions catalyzed by 3-PG dehydrogenase (SerA), phosphoserine aminotransferase (SerC) and phosphoserine phosphatase (SerB) ["
                      - link "62" [ref=e1494] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0310"
                        - generic [ref=e1495]: "62"
                      - text: "]. In order to enhance l-serine synthesis in the DPAW10C14–3, we used a mutant SerA that was resistant to feedback inhibition. Specifically, histidine 344, asparagine 346, and aspartate 364 in SerA were mutated to alanine (Patent CN 113621638). This modification improved D-PA titer to 6.80 g/L ("
                      - link "Fig. 5" [ref=e1496] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0025"
                        - generic [ref=e1497]: Fig. 5
                      - text: B). However, additional overexpression of
                      - emphasis [ref=e1498]: serC
                      - text: and
                      - emphasis [ref=e1499]: serB
                      - text: led to cell growth inhibition as well as D-PA production inhibitions. This could be attributed to the accumulation of l-serine (0.13 g/L in DPAW10C16 and 0.17 g/L in DPAW10C17, as shown in
                      - link "Fig. 5" [ref=e1500] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0025"
                        - generic [ref=e1501]: Fig. 5
                      - text: B), and similar toxic effects of serine accumulation have also been reported in previous studies [
                      - link "22" [ref=e1502] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0110"
                        - generic [ref=e1503]: "22"
                      - text: ","
                      - link "40" [ref=e1504] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0200"
                        - generic [ref=e1505]: "40"
                      - text: ","
                      - link "63" [ref=e1506] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0315"
                        - generic [ref=e1507]: "63"
                      - text: "]."
                    - generic [ref=e1508]:
                      - text: To reduce l-serine toxicity and promote its utilization,
                      - emphasis [ref=e1509]: glyA
                      - text: was overexpressed in DPAW10C18, resulting in a lower l-serine concentration (0.11 g/L) and further achievement of D-PA titer to 6.77 g/L. Moreover,
                      - emphasis [ref=e1510]: sdaA
                      - text: (encoding serine deaminase) was deleted to reduce the diversion of serine, slightly increasing serine level by 0.01 g/L. Subsequently, an additional copy of pTrc-
                      - emphasis [ref=e1511]: glyA
                      - text: was expressed at the
                      - emphasis [ref=e1512]: sdaA
                      - text: locus to increase the metabolic flux through GlyA. This helped decrease l-serine to 0.09 g/L in DPAW10C20 (
                      - link "Fig. 5" [ref=e1513] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0025"
                        - generic [ref=e1514]: Fig. 5
                      - text: B) and its D-PA concentration (6.84 g/L) was superior to that of DPAW10C15.
                    - generic [ref=e1515]:
                      - text: However, excessive glycine accumulation (>0.1 g/L) also effected cell growth [
                      - link "22" [ref=e1516] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0110"
                        - generic [ref=e1517]: "22"
                      - text: "], shown in Fig. S5. To alleviate glycine toxicity,"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1518]: gcvTHP
                      - text: was overexpressed under the control of the trc promoter, which elevated OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1519]: "600"
                      - text: of 6.47 and slightly improved D-PA production (6.96 g/L), suggesting 5,10-MTHF regeneration had been continually enhanced.
                    - generic [ref=e1520]:
                      - text: In addition, the detection of residual extracellular L-cysteine (
                      - link "Fig. 5" [ref=e1521] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0025"
                        - generic [ref=e1522]: Fig. 5
                      - text: C) demonstrated that surplus l-serine was converted to L-cysteine, diverting carbon flux from the one‑carbon cycle. To minimize this unnecessary diversion,
                      - emphasis [ref=e1523]: cysE
                      - text: (encoding serine acetyltransferase) was downregulated through start codon weakening [
                      - link "64" [ref=e1524] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0320"
                        - generic [ref=e1525]: "64"
                      - text: "]. As a result, cysteine became undetectable and D-PA titer increased modestly to 7.05 g/L in DPAW10C21–2 with"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1526]: cysE
                      - superscript [ref=e1527]: TTG
                      - text: (
                      - link "Fig. 5" [ref=e1528] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#f0025"
                        - generic [ref=e1529]: Fig. 5
                      - text: C), indicating that preserved l-serine flux would serve for 5,10-MTHF synthesis. Further analysis showed that glycine was 0.13 g/L (~1.73 mM) in DPAW10C20, but decreased to 0.06 g/L (~0.80 mM) in DPAW10C21, which confirmed that ~0.93 mM one‑carbon units was produced theoretically. In DPAW10C21–1, glycine accumulated to 0.07 g/L (~0.93 mM) while cysteine dropped to 0.01 g/L (~0.08 mM), with 6.98 g/L D-PA produced. In DPAW10C21–2, glycine reached 0.08 g/L (~1.07 mM) but cysteine diversion (~0.25 mM) was eliminated. Thus, lowering glycine overflow improved one‑carbon supply, while eliminating cysteine diversion was more effective, as it influenced serine precursor availability and indirectly affected glycine accumulation.
                    - generic [ref=e1530]:
                      - text: Notably, exogenous addition of 0.08 g/L l-serine and 0.05 g/L L-glycine at the beginning of shake flask cultivation further improved D-PA titer to 7.16 g/L (Fig. S6), underscoring the importance of maintaining balanced cofactor regulation throughout the entire growth phase rather than only at the end of fermentation. This also implied that temporally controlled expression of cofactors in D-PA biosynthetic modules might promote better performance of engineered strain [
                      - link "58" [ref=e1531] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0290"
                        - generic [ref=e1532]: "58"
                      - text: "]."
                    - generic [ref=e1533]: In summary, these metabolic adjustments reached a favorable balance between cell growth and serine-glycine conversion flux, enhancing one‑carbon unit regeneration to maximize D-PA production. Also, it could not be denied that the engineered strain DPAW10C21–2 had achieved a cofactor-guided growth-production optimization and was currently under evaluation in bioreactor conditions to validate its fermentation capacity.
                    - generic [ref=e1534]:
                      - text: (A) Schematic diagram of reducing serine transfer and feedback inhibition, which facilitates glycine conversion as well as THF. Red genes indicate overexpression, blue genes mean mutation while the red crosses express path blockage. (B) Changes of D-PA production, cell growth and D-PA/OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1535]: "600"
                      - text: as well as concentrations of l-serine by engineering serine-glycine system from DPAW10C14–3 to DPAW10C20 in shake flask cultivation. (C) Changes of D-PA production, cell growth and D-PA/OD
                      - subscript [ref=e1536]: "600"
                      - text: as well as concentrations of l-serine and L-glycine by engineering one carbon unit diversion from DPAW10C20 to DPAW10C21–2 in shake flask cultivation.
                  - generic [ref=e1537]:
                    - heading "3.6. Evaluating the production potential of D-PA by fed-batch fermentation in a 5 L bioreactor" [level=3] [ref=e1538]
                    - generic [ref=e1539]:
                      - generic [ref=e1540]:
                        - text: In order to evaluate the fermentation performance of the best-performing strain identified in shake flask experiments, DPAW10C21–2 was subjected to 5 L fed-batch fermentations, as shown in Fig. S7. In addition, we introduced a previously developed plasmid P
                        - emphasis [ref=e1541]: bcsd*
                        - text: 2 (
                        - link "Table 1" [ref=e1542] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#t0005"
                          - generic [ref=e1543]: Table 1
                        - text: ) into DPAW10C21–2, resulting in DPAW10C22. The citrate synthase (CitA) from
                        - emphasis [ref=e1544]: Aspergillus niger
                        - text: and the tetracycline repressor protein TetR in this plasmid are expressed under the control of the temperature sensitive P
                        - subscript [ref=e1545]: R
                        - text: "-P"
                        - subscript [ref=e1546]: L
                        - text: promoter (active at 37 °C and above, but inactive below 37 °C). Meanwhile, an SsrA tag was fused to both CitA and TetR, thereby reducing their residual levels. Besides, genes in D-PA synthesis (
                        - emphasis [ref=e1547]: panB, panC, ilvD,
                        - text: and
                        - emphasis [ref=e1548]: alsS
                        - text: ) are driven by the P
                        - subscript [ref=e1549]: LtetO
                        - text: promoter, which is inhibited by TetR [
                        - link "24" [ref=e1550] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0120"
                          - generic [ref=e1551]: "24"
                        - text: "]. Relying on the high copy number and temperature sensitivity of P"
                        - emphasis [ref=e1552]: bcsd*
                        - text: 2, DPAW10C22 could enable temperature-controlled decoupling of cell growth and D-PA biosynthesis, which promoted rapid biomass accumulation at 37 °C, followed by high-yield D-PA synthesis upon reducing the temperature to 30 °C (
                        - link "Fig. 6" [ref=e1553] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0030"
                          - generic [ref=e1554]: Fig. 6
                        - text: A and Fig. S8).
                      - figure [ref=e1555]:
                        - generic [ref=e1556]:
                          - img "Fig. 6"
                          - list [ref=e1557]:
                            - listitem [ref=e1558]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download high-res image (1MB)" [ref=e1559] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr6_lrg.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1561]: "Download: Download high-res image (1MB)"
                            - listitem [ref=e1562]:
                              - 'link "Download: Download full-size image" [ref=e1563] [cursor=pointer]':
                                - /url: https://ars.els-cdn.com/content/image/1-s2.0-S1385894725100223-gr6.jpg
                                - generic [ref=e1565]: "Download: Download full-size image"
                        - paragraph [ref=e1568]: Fig. 6. The fermentation performance of the engineered strain DPAW10C22 and DPAW10C23 in 5 L fermentors using different strategies under DO feedback fermentations. The superscripts ATG, GTG, and TTG indicated changes in the start codons of the genes. Measurements were carried out using samples taken at 0, 6, 10, 14, 24, 28, 32, 36, 48, 52, 56, 60, 72, 76, 80, 84, 96, 100, 104, 108 and 120 h.
                    - generic [ref=e1569]:
                      - text: However, due to earlier modifications weakening the phosphotransferase system (PTS) and TCA in original DPAW10 [
                      - link "19" [ref=e1570] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0095"
                        - generic [ref=e1571]: "19"
                      - text: "], DPAW10C21–2 exhibited slow growth during the initial fermentation phase. Downregulation of both native citrate synthase"
                      - emphasis [ref=e1572]: gltA
                      - text: (with its start codon mutated to TTG) and
                      - emphasis [ref=e1573]: icd
                      - text: (with its native promoter replaced by P
                      - emphasis [ref=e1574]: flic
                      - text: ) restricted TCA flux and biomass accumulation after the temperature shift. As a result, citrate, a TCA intermediate, was supplemented at that point to partially restore TCA activity and sustain cell growth for D-PA production.
                    - generic [ref=e1575]:
                      - generic [ref=e1576]:
                        - text: Originally, initial experiments using 1.0 g/L citrate in the fermentation medium revealed improved glucose absorption. It was shown that 20 g/L glucose was consumed within 10 h, 4 h earlier than before (
                        - link "Fig. 6" [ref=e1577] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0030"
                          - generic [ref=e1578]: Fig. 6
                        - text: B). However, residual citrate (0.19 g/L) remained at the end of fermentation (Fig. S9), which could be due to limited citrate uptake under aerobic conditions, as the citrate transporter was inhibited by a transcription factor FNR in
                        - emphasis [ref=e1579]: E. coli
                        - text: "["
                        - link "65" [ref=e1580] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0325"
                          - generic [ref=e1581]: "65"
                        - text: "]. To address this, CitT, a citrate/proton symporter, was overexpressed to promote the utilization of exogenous citrate. Thus, during the temperature switch to 30 °C for D-PA production, citrate supplementation could partially restore TCA flux ["
                        - link "66" [ref=e1582] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0330"
                          - generic [ref=e1583]: "66"
                        - text: "]. In DPAW10C23, supplement of 1.0, 1.5, or 2.0 g/L citrate at the start of fermentation resulted in no detectable residual citrate (Fig. S9), confirming that CitT effectively enhanced citrate uptake. The increased intracellular citrate availability helped improve cell growth and D-PA production. With 1.0 g/L citrate supplement, DPAW10C23 reached a maximum OD"
                        - subscript [ref=e1584]: "600"
                        - text: of 48.8 and a peak D-PA titer of 114.1 g/L, higher than 45.3 of OD
                        - subscript [ref=e1585]: "600"
                        - text: and 108.4 g/L D-PA in DPAW10C22, respectively (
                        - link "Table 2" [ref=e1586] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#t0010"
                          - generic [ref=e1587]: Table 2
                        - text: ","
                        - link "Fig. 6" [ref=e1588] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0030"
                          - generic [ref=e1589]: Fig. 6
                        - text: C). Supplement with 1.5 g/L citrate further increased the D-PA titer to 117.6 g/L (OD
                        - subscript [ref=e1590]: "600"
                        - text: 51.4), whereas supplement with 2.0 g/L citrate helped produce 111.5 g/L D-PA (OD
                        - subscript [ref=e1591]: "600"
                        - text: 53.9), indicating that enhanced citrate import promoted both biomass accumulation and D-PA production. However, for understanding the detailed flux distributions, future studies could perform
                        - superscript [ref=e1592]: "13"
                        - text: C-labeling and metabolomics studies to quantitatively evaluate the impact of CitT expression on TCA flux.
                      - generic [ref=e1593]:
                        - paragraph [ref=e1596]: Table 2. Comparison of different citrate concentrations added in the temperature switch node on 5-l fed-batch fermentation of D-PA by DPAW10C23.

                    - generic [ref=e1641]:
                      - generic [ref=e1642]:
                        - text: On the other hand, given the strain's limited isoleucine biosynthetic capability caused by the weakened threonine deaminase [
                        - link "29" [ref=e1643] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0145"
                          - generic [ref=e1644]: "29"
                        - text: "], L-isoleucine (40 g/L) was supplemented (4–10 mL) in the feeding reservoir and delivered to the 5 L fermenter as feeding started, which further enhanced the growth of DPAW10C23 ("
                        - link "Table 3" [ref=e1645] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#t0015"
                          - generic [ref=e1646]: Table 3
                        - text: ). It seemed that higher isoleucine additions led to earlier peaks in OD
                        - subscript [ref=e1647]: "600"
                        - text: ", suggesting accelerated initial cell growth. However, excessive biomass accumulation likely resulted in rapid nutrient depletion and metabolic stress, accompanied with the accumulation of inhibitory by-products, which in turn caused an earlier decline in cell viability ["
                        - link "40" [ref=e1648] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0200"
                          - generic [ref=e1649]: "40"
                        - text: "]. Since vitamin biosynthesis often begins in the late exponential phase and increases further during the stationary phase ["
                        - link "67" [ref=e1650] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0335"
                          - generic [ref=e1651]: "67"
                        - text: "], maintaining appropriate biomass with a prolonged exponential and stationary phase is desirable. With the addition of 8 mL isoleucine, strain DPAW10C23 achieved a maximum D-PA titer of 124.3 g/L, an OD"
                        - subscript [ref=e1652]: "600"
                        - text: of 53.6, and a productivity of 1.15 g/L/h. Meanwhile, approximate 480 g glucose and 76.8 g β-alanine were supplemented, resulting in a high yield of 0.78 g/g (
                        - link "Fig. 6" [ref=e1653] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#f0030"
                          - generic [ref=e1654]: Fig. 6
                        - text: E).

                    - generic [ref=e1725]:
                      - text: The maximum yield was defined as the ratio of the peak D-PA concentration to the total glucose fed at that specific time. In addition to the carbon sources added in the fermentation medium, yeast extract (8 g/L, ~58 % C), tryptone (12 g/L, ~48 % C), betaine (2 g/L, ~51 % C), citrate (1.5 g/L, ~38 % C), isoleucine (0.4 g/L, ~55 % C), and vitamins all provided nutritional sources to support both cell growth and precursor supply. Based on the theoretical glucose equivalent analysis (assuming complete conversion of carbon into glucose), these components provide approximately 34–35 g/L glucose equivalents [
                      - link "8" [ref=e1726] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0040"
                        - generic [ref=e1727]: "8"
                      - text: ","
                      - link "68" [ref=e1728] [cursor=pointer]:
                        - /url: "#bb0340"
                        - generic [ref=e1729]: "68"
                      - text: "]. On the other hand, we estimated the total substrate consumption based on the decrease in feed volume. By this calculation, approximately 160 g/L glucose and 26 g/L β-alanine (about 26 g/L glucose equivalents) were consumed. Taken together, after subtracting about 50 g/L (considering the average fermentation OD"
                      - subscript [ref=e1730]: "600"
                      - text: kept at around 40) estimated for biomass production and maintenance energy, and assuming complete conversion of glucose carbon to D-PA with a theoretical maximum yield of 0.81 g/g, the theoretical D-PA titer could reach about 138 g/L from relative estimated 170 g/L glucose (34 + 160 + 26–50 = 170), which confirmed that the total imputed carbon and nutritional chemicals would still support the production of 125 g/L D-PA. Moreover, the combination of the temperature sensitive plasmid and
                      - emphasis [ref=e1731]: icd
                      - text: modifications enhanced carbon redistribution, improving carbon efficiency during production and partially explaining our yield.
                    - generic [ref=e1732]:
                      - generic [ref=e1733]:
                        - text: Since D-PA biosynthesis requires β-alanine, most studies add it externally during D-PA fermentation [
                        - link "19" [ref=e1734] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0095"
                          - generic [ref=e1735]: "19"
                        - text: ","
                        - link "22" [ref=e1736] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0110"
                          - generic [ref=e1737]: "22"
                        - text: ","
                        - link "23" [ref=e1738] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0115"
                          - generic [ref=e1739]: "23"
                        - text: "]. Therefore, we evaluated and optimized β-alanine supplementation. However, supplying high concentration of β-alanine at 60 g/L and 80 g/L ("
                        - link "Table 4" [ref=e1740] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#t0020"
                          - generic [ref=e1741]: Table 4
                        - text: ) failed to further enhance D-PA production and cell growth. Residual β-alanine remained in the fermentation broth, indicating absorption inefficiency or possible PanC activity limitations, which suggested that future modification might benefit from strengthening the β-alanine transporter (CycA) and enhancing PanC catalytic activities [
                        - link "69" [ref=e1742] [cursor=pointer]:
                          - /url: "#bb0345"
                          - generic [ref=e1743]: "69"
                        - text: "]."

                    - generic [ref=e1797]: In addition, to exclude the influence of the fermentation medium and the fermentation strategy, we carried out the fermentations of DPAW11 under the same fermentation conditions as that of DPAW10C23 fermentations (Fig. S9). As the D-PA titer of DPAW11 increased only slightly from 97.2 to 99.1 g/L, which indicated that the D-PA production improvement for DPAW10C23 mainly resulted from cofactor-centric engineering and energy flux optimization.
                    - generic [ref=e1798]: Generally, these results demonstrated the successful implementation of cofactor-centric metabolic engineering for high-level D-PA production. Through strategic regulation of redox cofactors, energy metabolism and precursor availability, this study achieved the highest reported D-PA titer and glucose-to-D-PA conversion rate to date under scalable fermentation conditions. Meanwhile, synergetic cofactor engineering with flux distribution optimizations as a powerful tool for constructing industrial strain, laid a valuable foundation for large-scale microbial biosynthesis of relative compounds.
                    - generic [ref=e1799]: (A) Schematic diagram of pathway modifications related to glucose, citrate, isoleucine, and β-alanine. (B) Fermentation of the strain DPAW10C22 was carried out using basic initial and supplemented medium. After 6 h at 37 °C, it was transferred to constant temperature at 30 °C for the rest cultivation. (C) Fermentation of strain DPAW10C22 was carried out in basic medium supplemented with 1.0 g/L citrate. (D) Fermentation of strain DPAW10C23 was carried out in basic medium supplemented with 1.5 g/L citrate. (E) Fermentation of strain DPAW10C23 was carried out in basic medium supplemented with 1.5 g/L citrate and 8 mL of 40 g/L isoleucine was added to the feeding medium.
                    - generic [ref=e1800]: 2-KBY, 2-Ketobutyric acid.
                - generic [ref=e1801]:
                  - heading "4. Conclusions" [level=2] [ref=e1802]
                  - generic [ref=e1803]:
                    - text: This study confirmed that coordinating cofactor metabolism was pivotal for constructing high-efficient industrializing strains for high-value chemical biosynthesis. By integrating
                    - emphasis [ref=e1804]: in silico
                    - text: flux analysis with multi-module engineering of NADPH, ATP, and 5,10-MTHF regeneration systems, we first established a synergistic framework resolving the long-standing triad of cofactor limitations including redox imbalance, energy deficit and C1-unit scarcity. Meanwhile, the core innovation, a dynamically regulated redox-energy coupling module, enabled efficient ATP generation while eliminating NADH accumulation, serving as a fundamental constraint in high-density fermentation. Ultimately, our cofactor-centric engineering achieved record D-PA productivity (124.3 g/L titer and 0.78 g/g glucose conversion) in 5 L fed-batch fermentation, far surpassing the yields reported in earlier studies.
                  - generic [ref=e1805]:
                    - text: Nevertheless, the engineering of cofactors remained inherently challenging due to their rapid turnover and difficulties in real-time quantification [
                    - link "70" [ref=e1806] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#bb0350"
                      - generic [ref=e1807]: "70"
                    - text: ","
                    - link "71" [ref=e1808] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#bb0355"
                      - generic [ref=e1809]: "71"
                    - text: "]. Future efforts should focus on dynamic regulation systems and biosensors for monitoring and feedback control of cofactor levels, supported by proteomics or enzyme activity analyses. To this end, development of these would not only further improve the robustness of microbial cell factories but also expand the synthetic biology for industrial-scale synthesis of value-added compounds."
